# S5.2 P2 Paper 01 — WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension

## Metadata

- **Title:** WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension
- **Year:** 2021
- **Verified venue/status:** EMNLP 2021 main conference
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** ACL Anthology confirms EMNLP 2021 proceedings, pp. 4173–4185.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** webpage structural reading comprehension
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `websrcwebbasedstructural2021`

---

## Simple understanding

WebSRC introduces a dataset for web-based structural reading comprehension. It asks models to answer questions over webpages while using structural clues from HTML, layout, tables, links, and page organization.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Evaluate reading comprehension where the answer depends not only on text but also on webpage structure.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
webpage structural reading comprehension
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Webpage understanding requires modeling structural relations, not only extracting flat text from a page.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Dataset design for web-based reading comprehension.**
- **Use of webpage structural information.**
- **Comparison of models using structural and textual signals.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** It is a reading-comprehension dataset, not a full interactive web-agent benchmark.
- **Limitation 2:** It does not evaluate browser actions such as click/type/scroll.
- **Limitation 3:** It predates modern LLM/VLM agents, so its architecture assumptions are older.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is a safe source for stronger thesis claims because the verified venue/status is **EMNLP 2021 main conference**.

For final thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed venue → can support stronger claims
arXiv / technical report → useful for recent trends and system ideas
unclear venue → verify DOI/proceedings before final bibliography
course/report source → use only as informal background, not strong evidence
```

---

## Relation to S5.2

This paper belongs in **S5.2** because S5.2 discusses the perception and representation layer of LLM-based agents.

Its role is:

```text
WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension
→ webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
→ P2 support for perception / representation / grounding discussion
```

Use it after explaining what the paper actually contributes.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce the representation problem first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because generalized web automation requires agents to transform messy digital environments into usable state representations.

For web/data-extraction agents, this means:

```text
webpage / UI / screenshot / DOM / HTML
→ preserve useful semantics and structure
→ identify relevant elements or data
→ ground actions and extraction targets
→ act or extract reliably
```

This paper supports that pipeline by improving:

```text
webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
```

---

# S5.2 P2 Paper 02 — SYNAPSE: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control

## Metadata

- **Title:** SYNAPSE: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** ICLR 2024 conference paper
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** Published as a conference paper at ICLR 2024.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** trajectory exemplars / memory for computer control
- **S5.2 role:** memory/state representation for GUI interaction
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `synapsetrajectoryexemplarprompting2024`

---

## Simple understanding

SYNAPSE uses trajectories as exemplars to help LLM agents perform computer-control tasks. It adds memory and few-shot trajectory reuse so the model can imitate or adapt prior successful interaction patterns.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Prompt an agent with relevant previous trajectories so it can solve new computer-control tasks more effectively.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
trajectory exemplars / memory for computer control
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Trajectory exemplars and memory can improve LLM-based computer control, especially when the task resembles past interaction traces.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Trajectory-as-exemplar prompting mechanism.**
- **Memory/retrieval component.**

Also extract, if available:

- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Exemplar retrieval quality strongly affects behavior.
- **Limitation 2:** The method can overfit to demonstrations and fail under UI changes.
- **Limitation 3:** Computer-control success in benchmark environments does not guarantee robust open-web automation.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

# S5.2 P2 Paper 03 — Hierarchical Multimodal Pre-training for Visually Rich Webpage Understanding / WebLM

## Metadata

- **Title:** Hierarchical Multimodal Pre-training for Visually Rich Webpage Understanding / WebLM
- **Year:** 2024

- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents

- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `hierarchicalmultimodalpretraining2024`

---

## Simple understanding

WebLM proposes hierarchical multimodal pre-training for visually rich webpage understanding. It treats webpages as multimodal documents with text, layout, visual regions, and hierarchy.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

Pretrain models to understand webpages using hierarchical multimodal structure rather than plain text alone.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
visually rich webpage understanding
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
```

# S5.2 P2 Paper 04 — Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation / DUAL-VCR

## Metadata

- **Title:** Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation / DUAL-VCR
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** CVPR 2024
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** CVF open-access paper and DOI/conference records found.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** dual-view visual contextualization for web navigation
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `dualviewvisualcontextualization2024`

---

## Simple understanding

DUAL-VCR improves web navigation by giving models two complementary visual views: local element-level context and broader page-level context.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Use dual visual contextualization to help agents ground and choose actions in webpages.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
dual-view visual contextualization for web navigation
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Combining local and global visual views improves navigation decisions compared with relying on one visual representation only.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

- **Web navigation evaluation.**
- **Ablation comparing local/global visual context.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,

- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Visual contextualization can be expensive.
- **Limitation 2:** The approach depends on accurate element localization and page rendering.
- **Limitation 3:** It supports navigation but does not fully solve long-horizon recovery or verification.

General thesis-level limitation:

# S5.2 P2 Paper 05 — Ferret-UI: Grounded Mobile UI Understanding with Multimodal LLMs

## Metadata

- **Title:** Ferret-UI: Grounded Mobile UI Understanding with Multimodal LLMs
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** ECCV 2024
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** Springer/ECCV metadata found.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** grounded mobile UI understanding
- **S5.2 role:** screen/GUI perception and grounding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `ferretuigroundedmobile2024`

---

## Simple understanding

Ferret-UI extends multimodal LLMs for grounded mobile UI understanding. It focuses on identifying and reasoning about UI elements in mobile screenshots.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Adapt grounded multimodal LLM techniques to mobile UI screens so models can understand and locate interface elements.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
grounded mobile UI understanding
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Grounded UI understanding is necessary for mobile agents because mobile screens contain dense, small, and semantically rich UI elements.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Grounded mobile UI task formulation.**
- **Model adaptation for UI elements.**
- **Evaluation on mobile UI understanding/grounding tasks.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Mobile UI grounding is not identical to web or desktop grounding.
- **Limitation 2:** It focuses on perception/understanding, not a complete autonomous agent loop.
- **Limitation 3:** Performance depends on UI annotation quality and screen diversity.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

---

# S5.2 P2 Paper 09 — ScreenAI: A Vision-Language Model for UI and Infographics Understanding

## Metadata

- **Title:** ScreenAI: A Vision-Language Model for UI and Infographics Understanding
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** IJCAI 2024
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** IJCAI proceedings and ACM DOI page confirm IJCAI 2024.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** screen/UI and infographic understanding
- **S5.2 role:** screen/GUI perception and grounding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `screenaivisionlanguagemodel2024`

---

## Simple understanding

ScreenAI is a vision-language model designed for UI and infographic understanding. It targets screens as a special visual domain, where text, layout, icons, widgets, and charts matter together.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Build and evaluate a screen-specialized VLM for UI and infographic tasks.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
screen/UI and infographic understanding
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

General vision-language models benefit from screen-specific training because UI/infographic images differ from natural images.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **ScreenAI model/training setup.**
- **UI and infographic benchmark results.**
- **Examples of layout/text/chart understanding.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Screen understanding does not equal autonomous GUI interaction.
- **Limitation 2:** It may need additional grounding/action modules to become an agent.
- **Limitation 3:** Screen-specific datasets may not cover dynamic interaction states.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

- **Year:** 2025

- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `aguvisunifiedpurevision2025`

---

## Simple understanding

AGUVIS proposes unified pure-vision agents for autonomous GUI interaction. It reduces dependence on DOM/accessibility representations by making agents act mainly from visual inputs.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation

→ execution and verification
```

# S5.2 P2 Paper 12 — HtmlRAG: HTML is Better Than Plain Text for Modeling Retrieved Knowledge in RAG Systems

## Metadata

- **Title:** HtmlRAG: HTML is Better Than Plain Text for Modeling Retrieved Knowledge in RAG Systems
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** WWW 2025 / The ACM Web Conference
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** ACM DL and paper PDF confirm WWW 2025.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** HTML representation for web RAG
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `htmlraghtmlisbetter2025`

---

## Simple understanding

HtmlRAG argues that HTML is better than plain text for retrieved web knowledge in RAG systems because HTML preserves headings, tables, links, and semantic structure.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Use cleaned, compressed, and pruned HTML as the retrieved knowledge format for RAG instead of converting everything to plain text.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
HTML representation for web RAG
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

HTML preserves structural and semantic information that is lost in plain text conversion, and experiments on six QA datasets show advantages for HTML-based RAG.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,

# S5.2 P2 Paper 13 — DeskVision: Large Scale Desktop Region Captioning for Advanced GUI Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** DeskVision: Large Scale Desktop Region Captioning for Advanced GUI Agents
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2503.11170 / CoRR
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** desktop GUI region captioning and grounding data
- **S5.2 role:** screen/GUI perception and grounding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `deskvisionscaledesktopregion2025`

---

## Simple understanding

DeskVision introduces a large-scale desktop GUI region-captioning dataset and benchmark. It addresses the lack of desktop GUI data for training and evaluating GUI agents.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Use an automated pipeline, AutoCaptioner, to generate rich desktop GUI region captions and build DeskVision/DeskVision-Eval.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
desktop GUI region captioning and grounding data
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Large-scale desktop GUI data improves GUI grounding and understanding; GUIExplorer achieves strong results using DeskVision.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

# S5.2 P2 Paper 14 — Smoothing Grounding and Reasoning for MLLM-Powered GUI Agents with Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks

## Metadata

- **Title:** Smoothing Grounding and Reasoning for MLLM-Powered GUI Agents with Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2503.00401
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** query-oriented pivot between grounding and reasoning
- **S5.2 role:** screen/GUI perception and grounding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `smoothinggroundingreasoningmllm2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper proposes query inference as a pivot task between GUI grounding and action-oriented reasoning. Instead of only mapping queries to coordinates, the model learns to infer the intended query from action coordinates.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Bridge coordinate-oriented grounding and action-oriented reasoning with a query-oriented pivot task.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
query-oriented pivot between grounding and reasoning
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Query inference outperforms grounding under the same data scale and can match large-scale grounding-enhanced OS-Atlas with less than 0.1% of the training data.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

- **Priority:** P2

- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `screenspotproguigrounding2025`

---

## Simple understanding

ScreenSpot-Pro is a GUI grounding benchmark for professional high-resolution computer-use scenarios such as VSCode, AutoCAD, Photoshop, MATLAB, Office, and OS interfaces.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Evaluate whether MLLMs can ground natural-language instructions to precise UI locations in high-resolution professional applications.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
professional high-resolution GUI grounding benchmark
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Existing GUI grounding models perform poorly; the best model reaches only 18.9%, while ScreenSeekeR improves to 48.1% by narrowing the search area.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **1,581 instructions from 23 applications across five industries and three operating systems.**
- **Figures showing high-resolution distribution and small target-size difficulty.**
- **ScreenSeekeR visual search method and benchmark results.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** No confirmed accepted venue was found.
- **Limitation 2:** It evaluates grounding, not full task completion.
- **Limitation 3:** Professional software screenshots may not represent all consumer web/mobile tasks.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

# S5.2 P2 Paper 18 — Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2508.04412
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** DOM downsampling for web-agent snapshots
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `beyondpixelsexploringdom2025`

---

## Simple understanding

Beyond Pixels proposes D2Snap, a DOM downsampling algorithm for LLM-based web agents. It argues that DOM snapshots can be a strong alternative to screenshots if token length is reduced while preserving hierarchy.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Downsample DOM snapshots by consolidating DOM nodes instead of flattening/extracting elements.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
DOM downsampling for web-agent snapshots
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

D2Snap-downsampled DOM snapshots match a grounded GUI baseline at similar token scale and outperform it with slightly larger token budgets; DOM hierarchy is a useful UI feature for LLMs.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **D2Snap algorithm for DOM downsampling.**
- **67% success vs. 65% grounded GUI baseline at similar token order.**
- **Argument for DOM advantages: hierarchy, selectors, early access, and relative interaction.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** No confirmed accepted venue was found.
- **Limitation 2:** Evaluation is limited to sampled Online-Mind2Web tasks with GPT-4o backend.
- **Limitation 3:** DOM-based methods may still miss visual-only information.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

## Simple understanding

MGA is a memory-driven GUI agent that reframes interaction around observing first and then deciding. It uses an Observer, Memory Agent, Planner, and Grounding Agent.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
```

```text
memory-driven GUI interaction
```

# S5.2 P2 Paper 20 — From User Interface to Agent Interface: Efficiency Optimization of UI Representations for LLM Agents / UIFORMER

## Metadata

- **Title:** From User Interface to Agent Interface: Efficiency Optimization of UI Representations for LLM Agents / UIFORMER
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2512.13438
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** UI representation optimization / token efficiency
- **S5.2 role:** screen/GUI perception and grounding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `userinterfaceagentinterface2025`

---

## Simple understanding

UIFORMER treats UI representation optimization as a program synthesis problem. It aims to reduce token cost while preserving semantic completeness for LLM agents.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Automatically synthesize UI transformation programs that compress DOM/accessibility trees while preserving information needed for agent perception.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
UI representation optimization / token efficiency
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

UIFORMER reduces token usage by 48.7% to 55.8% across three UI navigation benchmarks while maintaining or improving agent performance.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Motivating study showing UI representation consumes 80–99% of agent-token costs.**
- **DSL-based transformation program synthesis.**
- **Evaluation across Android and Web benchmarks plus WeChat deployment.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** No confirmed accepted venue was found.
- **Limitation 2:** Program synthesis quality depends on rewards and DSL coverage.
- **Limitation 3:** Token reduction can still risk removing task-relevant UI context.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

---

## Venue/status caution

This should be cited as a preprint, technical report, or non-archival source unless a later peer-reviewed version is found.

For final thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed venue → can support stronger claims

unclear venue → verify DOI/proceedings before final bibliography
course/report source → use only as informal background, not strong evidence
```

# S5.2 P2 Paper 21 — CI4A: Semantic Component Interfaces for Agents Empowering Web Automation

## Metadata

- **Title:** CI4A: Semantic Component Interfaces for Agents Empowering Web Automation
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2601.14790 / CoRR
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** semantic component interfaces for web agents

- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `ci4asemanticcomponentinterfaces2026`

---

## Simple understanding

CI4A proposes semantic component interfaces for agents. Instead of forcing agents to operate through fragile low-level UI clicks, it exposes UI components as semantic tool primitives.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Transform UI components into a semantic triplet of state view, executable toolset, and interaction metadata so agents can call component-level tools.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
semantic component interfaces for web agents
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

A CI4A-based agent, Eous, achieves 86.3% success on a semantically reconstructed WebArena benchmark while reducing average interaction steps.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,

- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

# S5.2 P2 Paper 22 — See and Remember: A Multimodal Agent for Web Traversal / V-GEMS

## Metadata

- **Title:** See and Remember: A Multimodal Agent for Web Traversal / V-GEMS
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2603.02626
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** visual grounding + explicit memory for web traversal
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `seeremembermultimodalagent2026`

---

## Simple understanding

V-GEMS is a multimodal web traversal agent that combines adaptive visual perception, explicit URL-stack memory, and a symbolic counter.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Improve web traversal by adding visual grounding when text is insufficient, explicit state tracking for backtracking, and deterministic counting for quantitative tasks.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
visual grounding + explicit memory for web traversal
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

V-GEMS improves average success from 0.49 to 0.65 over WebWalker, a 28.7% relative gain, on the EverWebQA benchmark.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

## Source: `S5_2_P2\paper_first_notes\S5_2_P2_GLOBAL_SYNTHESIS.md`

# S5.2 P2 — Global Synthesis

## What this S5.2 P2 batch covers

The S5.2 P2 papers are mainly about the **perception and representation layer** of LLM-based web and GUI agents.

They cover:

1. Webpage structural reading comprehension.
2. Visually rich webpage understanding.
3. HTML/DOM representation for RAG and web agents.
4. DOM and UI representation compression.
5. Mobile/desktop/screen-specific VLMs.
6. GUI grounding and high-resolution professional UI grounding.
7. Pure-vision GUI agents.
8. Memory/state representation for GUI interaction.
9. Agent-native web/component interfaces.

## Main thesis value

This batch is important because generalized web automation depends on how the agent sees and represents the environment.

A strong S5.2 synthesis can be:

```text
Before an LLM-based agent can plan or act, it must represent the digital environment. 
Early approaches treated webpages as text or HTML, while more recent work uses multimodal screenshots, DOM/accessibility trees, UI-specific VLMs, grounding models, compressed UI representations, and semantic component interfaces. 
This evolution reflects a shift from passive webpage understanding toward action-oriented environment representations for agents.
```

## Recommended backbone sources

Use peer-reviewed papers as the stable backbone:

```text
WebSRC
SYNAPSE
WebLM / Hierarchical Multimodal Pre-training
DUAL-VCR
Ferret-UI
GUICourse
GUI-WORLD
ScreenAI
AGUVIS
InfiGUIAgent
HtmlRAG
```

## Use as recent trends / technical directions

Use these carefully as preprints or technical reports:

```text
CAAP
DeskVision
Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks
ScreenSpot-Pro
Phi-Ground
Beyond Pixels / D2Snap
MGA
UIFORMER
CI4A
V-GEMS
```

## Key writing structure for S5.2

A clean S5.2 structure can be:

1. Webpage understanding and HTML/DOM structure.
2. Visual and multimodal webpage understanding.
3. UI/screen-specific vision-language models.
4. GUI grounding and coordinate-level perception.
5. Representation compression and efficiency.
6. Memory/state representation for interaction.
7. Agent-native interfaces and semantic component protocols.

## Final recommendation

Keep all 22 papers as S5.2 P2 support. During writing, cite only the most relevant ones in each paragraph. Use the preprints mainly for emerging directions and recent trends.


---

# S5.2 P2 — Deep Venue / Status Verification Report

## Scope

- **Section:** S5.2 P2
- **Topic cluster:** webpage understanding, HTML/DOM representation, screen/UI understanding, GUI grounding, multimodal GUI/web agents, UI representation compression, and agent-facing interface design.
- **Papers checked:** 22
- **Method:** uploaded PDF metadata + targeted internet verification. If no accepted conference/journal/workshop record was found, the paper remains marked as arXiv/preprint/technical report.

## Summary counts

- **Confirmed peer-reviewed / archival:** 11
- **Still arXiv / preprint / technical report / non-archival:** 10
- **Unclear / needs manual DOI check:** 1

## Main confirmed peer-reviewed papers

- **WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension** → EMNLP 2021 main conference.
- **SYNAPSE: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control** → ICLR 2024 conference paper.
- **Hierarchical Multimodal Pre-training for Visually Rich Webpage Understanding / WebLM** → WSDM 2024.
- **Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation / DUAL-VCR** → CVPR 2024.
- **Ferret-UI: Grounded Mobile UI Understanding with Multimodal LLMs** → ECCV 2024, Springer LNCS proceedings.
- **GUICourse: From General Vision Language Model to Versatile GUI Agent** → ACL 2025 long paper.
- **GUI-WORLD: A Video Benchmark and Dataset for Multimodal GUI-oriented Understanding** → ICLR 2025.
- **ScreenAI: A Vision-Language Model for UI and Infographics Understanding** → IJCAI 2024.
- **AGUVIS: Unified Pure Vision Agents for Autonomous GUI Interaction** → ICML 2025 / PMLR 267.
- **InfiGUIAgent: A Multimodal Generalist GUI Agent with Native Reasoning and Reflection** → EACL 2026 long paper.
- **HtmlRAG: HTML is Better Than Plain Text for Modeling Retrieved Knowledge in RAG Systems** → WWW 2025 / The ACM Web Conference.

## Still preprint / technical report / no confirmed accepted venue found

- **CAAP: Context-Aware Action Planning Prompting to Solve Computer Tasks with Front-End UI Only** → arXiv 2406.06947; ICLR 2025 under-review/submission found. **Action:** Keep as preprint/submission.
- **DeskVision: Large Scale Desktop Region Captioning for Advanced GUI Agents** → arXiv 2503.11170 / CoRR. **Action:** Keep as preprint.
- **Smoothing Grounding and Reasoning for MLLM-Powered GUI Agents with Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks** → arXiv 2503.00401. **Action:** Keep as preprint.
- **Multimodal LLM Agents: Exploring LLM interactions in Software, Web and Operating Systems** → Course/project survey report. **Action:** Keep as non-archival course/report source.
- **ScreenSpot-Pro: GUI Grounding for Professional High-Resolution Computer Use** → arXiv 2504.07981 / OpenReview submission page. **Action:** Keep as preprint/benchmark.
- **Phi-Ground Tech Report: Advancing Perception in GUI Grounding** → arXiv 2507.23779 / Microsoft technical report. **Action:** Keep as technical report/preprint.
- **Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents** → arXiv 2508.04412. **Action:** Keep as preprint.
- **MGA: Memory-Driven GUI Agent for Observation-Centric Interaction** → CoRR arXiv 2510.24168; uploaded copy claims WSDM '25 but DBLP lists CoRR. **Action:** Use cautiously; verify DOI before strong citation.
- **From User Interface to Agent Interface: Efficiency Optimization of UI Representations for LLM Agents / UIFORMER** → arXiv 2512.13438. **Action:** Keep as preprint.
- **CI4A: Semantic Component Interfaces for Agents Empowering Web Automation** → arXiv 2601.14790 / CoRR. **Action:** Keep as preprint.
- **See and Remember: A Multimodal Agent for Web Traversal / V-GEMS** → arXiv 2603.02626. **Action:** Keep as preprint.

## Recommended citation strategy for S5.2

### Stronger backbone sources

Use the confirmed peer-reviewed papers as the backbone for S5.2:

- WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension — EMNLP 2021 main conference
- SYNAPSE: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control — ICLR 2024 conference paper
- Hierarchical Multimodal Pre-training for Visually Rich Webpage Understanding / WebLM — WSDM 2024
- Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation / DUAL-VCR — CVPR 2024
- Ferret-UI: Grounded Mobile UI Understanding with Multimodal LLMs — ECCV 2024, Springer LNCS proceedings
- GUICourse: From General Vision Language Model to Versatile GUI Agent — ACL 2025 long paper
- GUI-WORLD: A Video Benchmark and Dataset for Multimodal GUI-oriented Understanding — ICLR 2025
- ScreenAI: A Vision-Language Model for UI and Infographics Understanding — IJCAI 2024
- AGUVIS: Unified Pure Vision Agents for Autonomous GUI Interaction — ICML 2025 / PMLR 267
- InfiGUIAgent: A Multimodal Generalist GUI Agent with Native Reasoning and Reflection — EACL 2026 long paper
- HtmlRAG: HTML is Better Than Plain Text for Modeling Retrieved Knowledge in RAG Systems — WWW 2025 / The ACM Web Conference

### Use cautiously as recent-trend / technical evidence

Use the following as recent trend, technical-report, or emerging-direction evidence. Do not rely on them alone for strong thesis claims:

- CAAP: Context-Aware Action Planning Prompting to Solve Computer Tasks with Front-End UI Only — arXiv 2406.06947; ICLR 2025 under-review/submission found
- DeskVision: Large Scale Desktop Region Captioning for Advanced GUI Agents — arXiv 2503.11170 / CoRR
- Smoothing Grounding and Reasoning for MLLM-Powered GUI Agents with Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks — arXiv 2503.00401
- Multimodal LLM Agents: Exploring LLM interactions in Software, Web and Operating Systems — Course/project survey report
- ScreenSpot-Pro: GUI Grounding for Professional High-Resolution Computer Use — arXiv 2504.07981 / OpenReview submission page
- Phi-Ground Tech Report: Advancing Perception in GUI Grounding — arXiv 2507.23779 / Microsoft technical report
- Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents — arXiv 2508.04412
- MGA: Memory-Driven GUI Agent for Observation-Centric Interaction — CoRR arXiv 2510.24168; uploaded copy claims WSDM '25 but DBLP lists CoRR
- From User Interface to Agent Interface: Efficiency Optimization of UI Representations for LLM Agents / UIFORMER — arXiv 2512.13438
- CI4A: Semantic Component Interfaces for Agents Empowering Web Automation — arXiv 2601.14790 / CoRR
- See and Remember: A Multimodal Agent for Web Traversal / V-GEMS — arXiv 2603.02626

## Interpretation for S5.2

S5.2 P2 is a strong supporting batch for the representation/perception layer of LLM-based web and GUI agents. It shows a clear evolution:

```text
HTML/DOM structural understanding
→ multimodal webpage understanding
→ screen/UI-specific VLMs
→ GUI grounding and high-resolution perception
→ pure-vision GUI agents
→ representation compression and agent-native interfaces
```

For thesis writing, use peer-reviewed works such as WebSRC, WebLM/WSDM, DUAL-VCR/CVPR, Ferret-UI/ECCV, ScreenAI/IJCAI, HtmlRAG/WWW, AGUVIS/ICML, GUICourse/ACL, GUI-WORLD/ICLR, and InfiGUIAgent/EACL as the stable citation backbone. Use newer arXiv papers such as CI4A, UIFormer, V-GEMS, Phi-Ground, and DOM downsampling as emerging research directions.

## Recommended citation strategy for S5.2

### Stronger backbone sources

Use the confirmed peer-reviewed papers as the backbone for S5.2:

- WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension — EMNLP 2021 main conference
- SYNAPSE: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control — ICLR 2024 conference paper
- Hierarchical Multimodal Pre-training for Visually Rich Webpage Understanding / WebLM — WSDM 2024
- Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation / DUAL-VCR — CVPR 2024
- Ferret-UI: Grounded Mobile UI Understanding with Multimodal LLMs — ECCV 2024, Springer LNCS proceedings
- GUICourse: From General Vision Language Model to Versatile GUI Agent — ACL 2025 long paper
- GUI-WORLD: A Video Benchmark and Dataset for Multimodal GUI-oriented Understanding — ICLR 2025
- ScreenAI: A Vision-Language Model for UI and Infographics Understanding — IJCAI 2024
- AGUVIS: Unified Pure Vision Agents for Autonomous GUI Interaction — ICML 2025 / PMLR 267
- InfiGUIAgent: A Multimodal Generalist GUI Agent with Native Reasoning and Reflection — EACL 2026 long paper
- HtmlRAG: HTML is Better Than Plain Text for Modeling Retrieved Knowledge in RAG Systems — WWW 2025 / The ACM Web Conference

### Use cautiously as recent-trend / technical evidence

Use the following as recent trend, technical-report, or emerging-direction evidence. Do not rely on them alone for strong thesis claims:

- CAAP: Context-Aware Action Planning Prompting to Solve Computer Tasks with Front-End UI Only — arXiv 2406.06947; ICLR 2025 under-review/submission found
- DeskVision: Large Scale Desktop Region Captioning for Advanced GUI Agents — arXiv 2503.11170 / CoRR
- Smoothing Grounding and Reasoning for MLLM-Powered GUI Agents with Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks — arXiv 2503.00401
- Multimodal LLM Agents: Exploring LLM interactions in Software, Web and Operating Systems — Course/project survey report
- ScreenSpot-Pro: GUI Grounding for Professional High-Resolution Computer Use — arXiv 2504.07981 / OpenReview submission page
- Phi-Ground Tech Report: Advancing Perception in GUI Grounding — arXiv 2507.23779 / Microsoft technical report
- Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents — arXiv 2508.04412
- MGA: Memory-Driven GUI Agent for Observation-Centric Interaction — CoRR arXiv 2510.24168; uploaded copy claims WSDM '25 but DBLP lists CoRR
- From User Interface to Agent Interface: Efficiency Optimization of UI Representations for LLM Agents / UIFORMER — arXiv 2512.13438
- CI4A: Semantic Component Interfaces for Agents Empowering Web Automation — arXiv 2601.14790 / CoRR
- See and Remember: A Multimodal Agent for Web Traversal / V-GEMS — arXiv 2603.02626

## Interpretation for S5.2

S5.2 P2 is a strong supporting batch for the representation/perception layer of LLM-based web and GUI agents. It shows a clear evolution:

```text
HTML/DOM structural understanding
→ multimodal webpage understanding
→ screen/UI-specific VLMs
→ GUI grounding and high-resolution perception
→ pure-vision GUI agents
→ representation compression and agent-native interfaces
```

For thesis writing, use peer-reviewed works such as WebSRC, WebLM/WSDM, DUAL-VCR/CVPR, Ferret-UI/ECCV, ScreenAI/IJCAI, HtmlRAG/WWW, AGUVIS/ICML, GUICourse/ACL, GUI-WORLD/ICLR, and InfiGUIAgent/EACL as the stable citation backbone. Use newer arXiv papers such as CI4A, UIFormer, V-GEMS, Phi-Ground, and DOM downsampling as emerging research directions.

# S5.2 P2 Paper 01 — WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension

## Metadata

- **Title:** WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension
- **Year:** 2021
- **Verified venue/status:** EMNLP 2021 main conference
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** ACL Anthology confirms EMNLP 2021 proceedings, pp. 4173–4185.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** webpage structural reading comprehension
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `websrcwebbasedstructural2021`

---

## Simple understanding

WebSRC introduces a dataset for web-based structural reading comprehension. It asks models to answer questions over webpages while using structural clues from HTML, layout, tables, links, and page organization.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Evaluate reading comprehension where the answer depends not only on text but also on webpage structure.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
webpage structural reading comprehension
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Webpage understanding requires modeling structural relations, not only extracting flat text from a page.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Dataset design for web-based reading comprehension.**
- **Use of webpage structural information.**
- **Comparison of models using structural and textual signals.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** It is a reading-comprehension dataset, not a full interactive web-agent benchmark.
- **Limitation 2:** It does not evaluate browser actions such as click/type/scroll.
- **Limitation 3:** It predates modern LLM/VLM agents, so its architecture assumptions are older.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is a safe source for stronger thesis claims because the verified venue/status is **EMNLP 2021 main conference**.

For final thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed venue → can support stronger claims
arXiv / technical report → useful for recent trends and system ideas
unclear venue → verify DOI/proceedings before final bibliography
course/report source → use only as informal background, not strong evidence
```

---

## Relation to S5.2

This paper belongs in **S5.2** because S5.2 discusses the perception and representation layer of LLM-based agents.

Its role is:

```text
WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension
→ webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
→ P2 support for perception / representation / grounding discussion
```

Use it after explaining what the paper actually contributes.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce the representation problem first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because generalized web automation requires agents to transform messy digital environments into usable state representations.

For web/data-extraction agents, this means:

```text
webpage / UI / screenshot / DOM / HTML
→ preserve useful semantics and structure
→ identify relevant elements or data
→ ground actions and extraction targets
→ act or extract reliably
```

This paper supports that pipeline by improving:

```text
webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
```

---

## How to use this paper in the literature review

Recommended use:

- Use it as **P2 support** in S5.2.
- Extract one precise representation/perception contribution.
- Extract one limitation or failure mode.
- Compare it with nearby works on HTML/DOM, visual screenshots, GUI grounding, or agent-facing interfaces.
- If the paper is a preprint/technical report, phrase claims cautiously.

Suggested thesis sentence:

> WebSRC: A Dataset for Web-Based Structural Reading Comprehension contributes to S5.2 by addressing **webpage structural reading comprehension**, showing that the quality of environment representation strongly affects the reliability of LLM-based web and GUI agents.

---

## Comparison with nearby papers

Compare this paper with:

```text
WebSRC / WebLM / DUAL-VCR
HtmlRAG / DOM downsampling / UIFORMER
ScreenAI / Ferret-UI / GUICourse / GUI-WORLD
ScreenSpot-Pro / Phi-Ground / query-oriented pivot tasks
AGUVIS / InfiGUIAgent / MGA / V-GEMS
CI4A and agent-native interface proposals
```

```text

```

---

## Reading decision

- **Keep in S5.2 P2:** Yes
- **Read fully?** Yes, if it becomes central to the representation/perception subsection.
- **Depth needed:** Medium to high
- **Main use:** Support discussion of webpage structural reading comprehension
- **Most important parts to read:**
  - Abstract and introduction
  - Representation/perception method
  - Main architecture or benchmark figure
  - Dataset/benchmark construction
  - Main results table
  - Ablation and limitations

---

# S5.2 P2 Paper 02 — SYNAPSE: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control

## Metadata

- **Title:** SYNAPSE: Trajectory-as-Exemplar Prompting with Memory for Computer Control
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** ICLR 2024 conference paper
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** Published as a conference paper at ICLR 2024.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** trajectory exemplars / memory for computer control
- **S5.2 role:** memory/state representation for GUI interaction
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `synapsetrajectoryexemplarprompting2024`

---

## Simple understanding

SYNAPSE uses trajectories as exemplars to help LLM agents perform computer-control tasks. It adds memory and few-shot trajectory reuse so the model can imitate or adapt prior successful interaction patterns.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Prompt an agent with relevant previous trajectories so it can solve new computer-control tasks more effectively.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
trajectory exemplars / memory for computer control
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Trajectory exemplars and memory can improve LLM-based computer control, especially when the task resembles past interaction traces.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

Also extract, if available:

- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Exemplar retrieval quality strongly affects behavior.
- **Limitation 2:** The method can overfit to demonstrations and fail under UI changes.
- **Limitation 3:** Computer-control success in benchmark environments does not guarantee robust open-web automation.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

# S5.2 P2 Paper 04 — Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation / DUAL-VCR

## Metadata

- **Title:** Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation / DUAL-VCR
- **Year:** 2024
- **Verified venue/status:** CVPR 2024
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** CVF open-access paper and DOI/conference records found.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** dual-view visual contextualization for web navigation
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `dualviewvisualcontextualization2024`

---

## Simple understanding

DUAL-VCR improves web navigation by giving models two complementary visual views: local element-level context and broader page-level context.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Use dual visual contextualization to help agents ground and choose actions in webpages.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
dual-view visual contextualization for web navigation
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Combining local and global visual views improves navigation decisions compared with relying on one visual representation only.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Dual-view representation design.**
- **Web navigation evaluation.**
- **Ablation comparing local/global visual context.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Visual contextualization can be expensive.
- **Limitation 2:** The approach depends on accurate element localization and page rendering.
- **Limitation 3:** It supports navigation but does not fully solve long-horizon recovery or verification.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is a safe source for stronger thesis claims because the verified venue/status is **CVPR 2024**.

For final thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed venue → can support stronger claims
arXiv / technical report → useful for recent trends and system ideas

course/report source → use only as informal background, not strong evidence
```

---

Its role is:

```text
Dual-View Visual Contextualization for Web Navigation / DUAL-VCR

```

Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce the representation problem first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because generalized web automation requires agents to transform messy digital environments into usable state representations.

For web/data-extraction agents, this means:

```text
webpage / UI / screenshot / DOM / HTML
→ preserve useful semantics and structure
→ identify relevant elements or data
→ ground actions and extraction targets
→ act or extract reliably
```

This paper supports that pipeline by improving:

```text
webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
```

# S5.2 P2 Paper 08 — GUI-WORLD: A Video Benchmark and Dataset for Multimodal GUI-oriented Understanding

## Metadata

- **Title:** GUI-WORLD: A Video Benchmark and Dataset for Multimodal GUI-oriented Understanding
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** ICLR 2025
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** OpenReview/ICLR and arXiv indicate accepted ICLR 2025.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** video GUI-oriented understanding benchmark
- **S5.2 role:** screen/GUI perception and grounding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `guiworldvideomultimodal2025`

---

## Simple understanding

GUI-WORLD provides a video benchmark and dataset for multimodal GUI-oriented understanding. Instead of only static screenshots, it evaluates temporal UI understanding from videos.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Benchmark how models understand GUI states, actions, and transitions over time from video data.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
video GUI-oriented understanding benchmark
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Temporal GUI understanding is important because agents often need to infer what happened across multiple UI states, not just inspect one screenshot.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Video-based GUI dataset design.**
- **Task categories for GUI-oriented understanding.**
- **Model results on temporal GUI tasks.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Video understanding is expensive and may not be necessary for all web tasks.
- **Limitation 2:** It evaluates understanding more than autonomous task completion.
- **Limitation 3:** Video datasets may not cover all real software/web workflows.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.
