# S5.2 P2 Paper 12 — HtmlRAG: HTML is Better Than Plain Text for Modeling Retrieved Knowledge in RAG Systems

## Metadata

- **Title:** HtmlRAG: HTML is Better Than Plain Text for Modeling Retrieved Knowledge in RAG Systems
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** WWW 2025 / The ACM Web Conference
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Venue note:** ACM DL and paper PDF confirm WWW 2025.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** HTML representation for web RAG
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `htmlraghtmlisbetter2025`

---

## Simple understanding

HtmlRAG argues that HTML is better than plain text for retrieved web knowledge in RAG systems because HTML preserves headings, tables, links, and semantic structure.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Use cleaned, compressed, and pruned HTML as the retrieved knowledge format for RAG instead of converting everything to plain text.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
HTML representation for web RAG
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

HTML preserves structural and semantic information that is lost in plain text conversion, and experiments on six QA datasets show advantages for HTML-based RAG.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Figure 1 showing information loss when HTML is converted to plain text.**
- **HTML cleaning/compression and block-tree pruning strategy.**
- **Experiments on six QA datasets.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** HTML is longer and noisier than plain text.
- **Limitation 2:** Cleaning and pruning can remove useful information if not tuned carefully.
- **Limitation 3:** It is RAG-focused, not a direct web-action agent paper.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

---

This is a safe source for stronger thesis claims because the verified venue/status is **WWW 2025 / The ACM Web Conference**.

# S5.2 P2 Paper 14 — Smoothing Grounding and Reasoning for MLLM-Powered GUI Agents with Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks

## Metadata

- **Title:** Smoothing Grounding and Reasoning for MLLM-Powered GUI Agents with Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2503.00401
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** query-oriented pivot between grounding and reasoning
- **S5.2 role:** screen/GUI perception and grounding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `smoothinggroundingreasoningmllm2025`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper proposes query inference as a pivot task between GUI grounding and action-oriented reasoning. Instead of only mapping queries to coordinates, the model learns to infer the intended query from action coordinates.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Bridge coordinate-oriented grounding and action-oriented reasoning with a query-oriented pivot task.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
query-oriented pivot between grounding and reasoning
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Query inference outperforms grounding under the same data scale and can match large-scale grounding-enhanced OS-Atlas with less than 0.1% of the training data.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,

- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,

# S5.2 P2 Paper 16 — ScreenSpot-Pro: GUI Grounding for Professional High-Resolution Computer Use

## Metadata

- **Title:** ScreenSpot-Pro: GUI Grounding for Professional High-Resolution Computer Use
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2504.07981 / OpenReview submission page
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint/benchmark.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** professional high-resolution GUI grounding benchmark
- **S5.2 role:** screen/GUI perception and grounding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `screenspotproguigrounding2025`

---

## Simple understanding

ScreenSpot-Pro is a GUI grounding benchmark for professional high-resolution computer-use scenarios such as VSCode, AutoCAD, Photoshop, MATLAB, Office, and OS interfaces.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Evaluate whether MLLMs can ground natural-language instructions to precise UI locations in high-resolution professional applications.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
professional high-resolution GUI grounding benchmark
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Existing GUI grounding models perform poorly; the best model reaches only 18.9%, while ScreenSeekeR improves to 48.1% by narrowing the search area.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **1,581 instructions from 23 applications across five industries and three operating systems.**
- **Figures showing high-resolution distribution and small target-size difficulty.**
- **ScreenSeekeR visual search method and benchmark results.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** No confirmed accepted venue was found.
- **Limitation 2:** It evaluates grounding, not full task completion.
- **Limitation 3:** Professional software screenshots may not represent all consumer web/mobile tasks.

```text

```

## Simple understanding

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.

# S5.2 P2 Paper 18 — Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** Beyond Pixels: Exploring DOM Downsampling for LLM-Based Web Agents
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2508.04412
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Venue note:** Keep as preprint.
- **Thesis section:** S5.2 — Perception, Representation, and Grounding for Web/GUI Agents
- **Main category:** DOM downsampling for web-agent snapshots
- **S5.2 role:** webpage/HTML/DOM representation and web-state understanding
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `beyondpixelsexploringdom2025`

---

## Simple understanding

Beyond Pixels proposes D2Snap, a DOM downsampling algorithm for LLM-based web agents. It argues that DOM snapshots can be a strong alternative to screenshots if token length is reduced while preserving hierarchy.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Downsample DOM snapshots by consolidating DOM nodes instead of flattening/extracting elements.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
DOM downsampling for web-agent snapshots
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

D2Snap-downsampled DOM snapshots match a grounded GUI baseline at similar token scale and outperform it with slightly larger token budgets; DOM hierarchy is a useful UI feature for LLMs.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **D2Snap algorithm for DOM downsampling.**
- **67% success vs. 65% grounded GUI baseline at similar token order.**
- **Argument for DOM advantages: hierarchy, selectors, early access, and relative interaction.**

Also extract, if available:

- dataset size,
- benchmark domains,
- representation format,
- model architecture or prompt strategy,
- compression/token reduction,
- grounding accuracy,
- task success rate,
- ablation results,
- and stated limitations.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** No confirmed accepted venue was found.
- **Limitation 2:** Evaluation is limited to sampled Online-Mind2Web tasks with GPT-4o backend.
- **Limitation 3:** DOM-based methods may still miss visual-only information.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better perception or representation is necessary but not sufficient.
A generalized web agent also needs planning, memory, action execution, error recovery, and evaluation.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support** for S5.2, not as the only foundation for the whole section.

## Simple understanding

MGA is a memory-driven GUI agent that reframes interaction around observing first and then deciding. It uses an Observer, Memory Agent, Planner, and Grounding Agent.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Compress past observations, thoughts, and actions into structured memory so each GUI step has context without replaying the full trajectory.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
memory-driven GUI interaction
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

## Key finding / main claim

Structured memory and observation-centric interaction improve robustness, generalization, and efficiency on OSWorld and desktop application tasks.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,
- excessive DOM/accessibility-tree token cost,
- poor GUI grounding for small UI elements,
- high-resolution professional interfaces,
- weak screen understanding in general VLMs,
- lack of UI-specific training data,
- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

# S5.2 P2 Paper 20 — From User Interface to Agent Interface: Efficiency Optimization of UI Representations for LLM Agents / UIFORMER

## Metadata

- **Title:** From User Interface to Agent Interface: Efficiency Optimization of UI Representations for LLM Agents / UIFORMER
- **Year:** 2025

- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `userinterfaceagentinterface2025`

---

## Simple understanding

UIFORMER treats UI representation optimization as a program synthesis problem. It aims to reduce token cost while preserving semantic completeness for LLM agents.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Automatically synthesize UI transformation programs that compress DOM/accessibility trees while preserving information needed for agent perception.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
UI representation optimization / token efficiency
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

UIFORMER reduces token usage by 48.7% to 55.8% across three UI navigation benchmarks while maintaining or improving agent performance.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?

# S5.2 P2 Paper 21 — CI4A: Semantic Component Interfaces for Agents Empowering Web Automation

## Metadata

- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2601.14790 / CoRR

- **BibTeX key:** `ci4asemanticcomponentinterfaces2026`

---

## Simple understanding

CI4A proposes semantic component interfaces for agents. Instead of forcing agents to operate through fragile low-level UI clicks, it exposes UI components as semantic tool primitives.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Transform UI components into a semantic triplet of state view, executable toolset, and interaction metadata so agents can call component-level tools.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
semantic component interfaces for web agents
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

A CI4A-based agent, Eous, achieves 86.3% success on a semantically reconstructed WebArena benchmark while reducing average interaction steps.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,

- high-resolution professional interfaces,

- missing memory/state representation,
- and mismatch between human-facing UI and agent-facing action needs.

## Metadata

- **Year:** 2026

---

## Simple understanding

V-GEMS is a multimodal web traversal agent that combines adaptive visual perception, explicit URL-stack memory, and a symbolic counter.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Agents need to understand webpages, screens, DOM trees, HTML, UI elements, or visual layouts before they can act.
Paper → This work improves representation, perception, grounding, compression, or interface design.
Goal → Make the environment state more usable for LLM/VLM agents.
```

For this section, the first goal is to understand **what the paper does for perception or representation**.  
Only after that, connect it to S5.2 and to generalized web automation.

---

## Core idea

Improve web traversal by adding visual grounding when text is insufficient, explicit state tracking for backtracking, and deterministic counting for quantitative tasks.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
visual grounding + explicit memory for web traversal
```

This corresponds to the environment-understanding layer of an agent:

```text
raw webpage / screenshot / UI tree / HTML / DOM
→ cleaned or structured representation
→ model perception and grounding
→ action planning
→ execution and verification
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

V-GEMS improves average success from 0.49 to 0.65 over WebWalker, a 28.7% relative gain, on the EverWebQA benchmark.

For S5.2, the important point is not only the final benchmark score.  
The key question is:

```text
What representation or perception bottleneck does this paper solve?
```

Common S5.2 bottlenecks include:

- loss of structure when HTML is converted to text,

- high-resolution professional interfaces,

Does this paper improve text/HTML/DOM representation, visual screen understanding, GUI grounding, memory/state representation, or agent-native interaction?
```

---

## Reading decision

- **Keep in S5.2 P2:** Yes
- **Read fully?** Selected sections are enough unless promoted later.
- **Depth needed:** Low to medium
- **Main use:** Support discussion of visual grounding + explicit memory for web traversal
- **Most important parts to read:**
  - Abstract and introduction
  - Representation/perception method
  - Main architecture or benchmark figure
  - Dataset/benchmark construction
  - Main results table
  - Ablation and limitations

---

## One-sentence summary

See and Remember: A Multimodal Agent for Web Traversal / V-GEMS is a P2 source for S5.2 because it helps explain **visual grounding + explicit memory for web traversal**, but it should be cited according to its verified venue/status and used mainly to enrich the perception/representation discussion.

## Source: `S5_2_P2_paper_first_detailed_markdown_notes\S5_2_P2_GLOBAL_SYNTHESIS.md`

# S5.2 P2 — Global Synthesis

## What this S5.2 P2 batch covers

The S5.2 P2 papers are mainly about the **perception and representation layer** of LLM-based web and GUI agents.

They cover:

1. Webpage structural reading comprehension.
2. Visually rich webpage understanding.
3. HTML/DOM representation for RAG and web agents.
4. DOM and UI representation compression.
5. Mobile/desktop/screen-specific VLMs.
6. GUI grounding and high-resolution professional UI grounding.
7. Pure-vision GUI agents.
8. Memory/state representation for GUI interaction.
9. Agent-native web/component interfaces.

## Main thesis value

This batch is important because generalized web automation depends on how the agent sees and represents the environment.

A strong S5.2 synthesis can be:

```text
Before an LLM-based agent can plan or act, it must represent the digital environment. 
Early approaches treated webpages as text or HTML, while more recent work uses multimodal screenshots, DOM/accessibility trees, UI-specific VLMs, grounding models, compressed UI representations, and semantic component interfaces. 
This evolution reflects a shift from passive webpage understanding toward action-oriented environment representations for agents.
```

## Recommended backbone sources

Use peer-reviewed papers as the stable backbone:

```text
WebSRC
SYNAPSE
WebLM / Hierarchical Multimodal Pre-training
DUAL-VCR
Ferret-UI
GUICourse
GUI-WORLD
ScreenAI
AGUVIS
InfiGUIAgent
HtmlRAG
```

## Use as recent trends / technical directions

Use these carefully as preprints or technical reports:

```text
CAAP
DeskVision
Query-Oriented Pivot Tasks
ScreenSpot-Pro
Phi-Ground
Beyond Pixels / D2Snap
MGA
UIFORMER
CI4A
V-GEMS
```

## Key writing structure for S5.2

A clean S5.2 structure can be:

1. Webpage understanding and HTML/DOM structure.
2. Visual and multimodal webpage understanding.
3. UI/screen-specific vision-language models.
4. GUI grounding and coordinate-level perception.
5. Representation compression and efficiency.
6. Memory/state representation for interaction.
7. Agent-native interfaces and semantic component protocols.

## Final recommendation

Keep all 22 papers as S5.2 P2 support. During writing, cite only the most relevant ones in each paragraph. Use the preprints mainly for emerging directions and recent trends.
